```
1	name: Pulumi Preview
2	
3	on:
4	  pull_request:
5	    paths:
6	      - "infrastructure/pulumi/**"
7	      - ".github/workflows/pulumi-preview.yml"
8	
9	env:
10	  CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN }}
11	  CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID: ${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID }}
12	  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: ${{ secrets.R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID }}
13	  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: ${{ secrets.R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY }}
14	  PULUMI_CONFIG_PASSPHRASE: ${{ secrets.PULUMI_CONFIG_PASSPHRASE }}
15	  SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME }}
16	  SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME: ${{ secrets.SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME }}
17	  SNOWFLAKE_USER: PULUMI_SVC
18	  SNOWFLAKE_AUTHENTICATOR: WORKLOAD_IDENTITY
19	  SNOWFLAKE_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER: OIDC
20	  SNOWFLAKE_ROLE: PULUMI_ROLE
21	
22	jobs:
23	  preview:
24	    name: Preview
25	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
26	    environment: pulumi
27	    permissions:
28	      id-token: write
29	      contents: read
30	      pull-requests: write
31	    defaults:
32	      run:
33	        working-directory: infrastructure/pulumi
34	
35	    steps:
36	      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
37	
38	      - uses: actions/setup-go@v5
39	        with:
40	          go-version: "1.24"
41	          cache-dependency-path: infrastructure/pulumi/go.sum
42	
43	      - name: Install dependencies
44	        run: go mod tidy
45	
46	      - name: Get Snowflake OIDC token
47	        run: |
48	          OIDC_TOKEN=$(curl -sS -H "Authorization: bearer $ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN" \
49	            "${ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL}&audience=snowflakecomputing.com" | jq -r '.value')
50	          if [ -z "$OIDC_TOKEN" ] || [ "$OIDC_TOKEN" = "null" ]; then
51	            echo "::error::Failed to retrieve Snowflake OIDC token"
52	            exit 1
53	          fi
54	          echo "::add-mask::$OIDC_TOKEN"
55	          echo "SNOWFLAKE_TOKEN=$OIDC_TOKEN" >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
56	
57	      - name: Login to R2 backend
58	        run: pulumi login 's3://pulumi?endpoint=https://${{ secrets.CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID }}.r2.cloudflarestorage.com&region=auto&s3ForcePathStyle=true'
59	
60	      - name: Select stack
61	        run: pulumi stack select dev
62	
63	      - name: Pulumi Preview
64	        id: preview
65	        run: |
66	          set +e
67	
68	          # Run preview with JSON output for parsing
69	          pulumi preview --json 2>/dev/null > preview.json
70	          JSON_EXIT=$?
71	
72	          # Also get human-readable output
73	          pulumi preview --diff 2>&1 | tee preview.txt
74	          PREVIEW_EXIT=${PIPESTATUS[0]}
75	
76	          echo "exit_code=$PREVIEW_EXIT" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
77	
78	          # Parse JSON for accurate counts
79	          if [ -f preview.json ] && [ -s preview.json ]; then
80	            CREATES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "create")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
81	            UPDATES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "update")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
82	            DELETES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "delete")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
83	            SAMES=$(jq '[.steps[]? | select(.op == "same")] | length' preview.json 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
84	          else
85	            # Fallback to text parsing
86	            CREATES=$(grep -c "^\s*+.*create" preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
87	            UPDATES=$(grep -c "^\s*~.*update" preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
88	            DELETES=$(grep -c "^\s*-.*delete" preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
89	            SAMES=$(grep -oP '\d+(?= unchanged)' preview.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "0")
90	          fi
91	
92	          echo "creates=$CREATES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
93	          echo "updates=$UPDATES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
94	          echo "deletes=$DELETES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
95	          echo "sames=$SAMES" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
96	
97	          # Determine result status
98	          TOTAL_CHANGES=$((CREATES + UPDATES + DELETES))
99	          if [ $PREVIEW_EXIT -ne 0 ]; then
100	            echo "result=failed" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
101	          elif [ $TOTAL_CHANGES -eq 0 ]; then
102	            echo "result=no-changes" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
103	          else
104	            echo "result=has-changes" >> $GITHUB_OUTPUT
105	          fi
106	
107	      - name: Generate PR Comment
108	        id: comment
109	        run: |
110	          RESULT="${{ steps.preview.outputs.result }}"
111	          CREATES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.creates }}"
112	          UPDATES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.updates }}"
113	          DELETES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.deletes }}"
114	          SAMES="${{ steps.preview.outputs.sames }}"
115	
116	          # Set header based on result
117	          case $RESULT in
118	            "failed")
119	              HEADER="## ❌ Pulumi Preview Failed"
120	              BADGE="https://img.shields.io/badge/pulumi-failed-red?style=flat-square&logo=pulumi"
121	              ;;
122	            "no-changes")
123	              HEADER="## ✅ Pulumi Preview: No Changes"
124	              BADGE="https://img.shields.io/badge/pulumi-no%20changes-green?style=flat-square&logo=pulumi"
125	              ;;
126	            "has-changes")
127	              HEADER="## 🔄 Pulumi Preview: Changes Detected"
128	              BADGE="https://img.shields.io/badge/pulumi-changes%20detected-blue?style=flat-square&logo=pulumi"
129	              ;;
130	          esac
131	
132	          # Build comment
133	          cat << 'COMMENT_EOF' > comment.md
134	          HEADER_PLACEHOLDER
135	
136	          ![Pulumi](BADGE_PLACEHOLDER)
137	
138	          ### 📊 Resource Changes
139	
140	          | | Create | Update | Delete | Unchanged |
141	          |:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|:-:|
142	          | Count | **CREATES_PLACEHOLDER** | **UPDATES_PLACEHOLDER** | **DELETES_PLACEHOLDER** | SAMES_PLACEHOLDER |
143	          | | 🆕 | 📝 | 🗑️ | ⏸️ |
144	
145	          COMMENT_EOF
146	
147	          # Add warning section for destructive changes
148	          if [ "$DELETES" -gt 0 ]; then
149	            cat << 'WARNING_EOF' >> comment.md
150	
151	          > [!CAUTION]
152	          > **DELETES_PLACEHOLDER resources will be deleted.** Please review carefully before merging.
153	
154	          WARNING_EOF
155	          fi
156	
157	          # Add details section
158	          cat << 'DETAILS_EOF' >> comment.md
159	
160	          <details>
161	          <summary>📋 Preview Details</summary>
162	
163	          ```diff
164	          DETAILS_EOF
165	
166	          # Add preview output (truncated if needed)
167	          head -c 60000 preview.txt >> comment.md
168	
169	          cat << 'END_EOF' >> comment.md
170	          ```
171	
172	          </details>
173	
174	          ---
175	
176	          <sub>🏗️ **Stack:** `dev` | 📁 **Path:** `infrastructure/pulumi` | 🔗 **Workflow:** [Run #${{ github.run_number }}](${{ github.server_url }}/${{ github.repository }}/actions/runs/${{ github.run_id }})</sub>
177	          END_EOF
178	
179	          # Replace placeholders
180	          sed -i "s|HEADER_PLACEHOLDER|$HEADER|g" comment.md
181	          sed -i "s|BADGE_PLACEHOLDER|$BADGE|g" comment.md
182	          sed -i "s|CREATES_PLACEHOLDER|$CREATES|g" comment.md
183	          sed -i "s|UPDATES_PLACEHOLDER|$UPDATES|g" comment.md
184	          sed -i "s|DELETES_PLACEHOLDER|$DELETES|g" comment.md
185	          sed -i "s|SAMES_PLACEHOLDER|$SAMES|g" comment.md
186	
187	      - name: Post PR Comment
188	        uses: actions/github-script@v7
189	        with:
190	          script: |
191	            const fs = require('fs');
192	            const body = fs.readFileSync('infrastructure/pulumi/comment.md', 'utf8');
193	
194	            const marker = '<!-- pulumi-preview-comment -->';
195	            const fullBody = marker + '\n' + body;
196	
197	            // Find existing comment
198	            const { data: comments } = await github.rest.issues.listComments({
199	              owner: context.repo.owner,
200	              repo: context.repo.repo,
201	              issue_number: context.issue.number,
202	            });
203	
204	            const existingComment = comments.find(c => c.body.includes(marker));
205	
206	            if (existingComment) {
207	              await github.rest.issues.updateComment({
208	                owner: context.repo.owner,
209	                repo: context.repo.repo,
210	                comment_id: existingComment.id,
211	                body: fullBody
212	              });
213	              console.log('Updated existing comment');
214	            } else {
215	              await github.rest.issues.createComment({
216	                owner: context.repo.owner,
217	                repo: context.repo.repo,
218	                issue_number: context.issue.number,
219	                body: fullBody
220	              });
221	              console.log('Created new comment');
222	            }
223	
224	      - name: Set exit code
225	        if: steps.preview.outputs.result == 'failed'
226	        run: exit 1
227	
```

> AGENT

`.env.example` を作成しました。使い方：

```bash
cd infrastructure/pulumi
cp .env.example .env.local
# 各値を埋めて保存
```

**必要な環境変数一覧：**

| 変数 | 用途 | 取得場所 |
|------|------|---------|
| `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` | Cloudflare リソース管理 | Dashboard → API Tokens（D1/R2/Workers 権限） |
| `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` | アカウント識別子 | Dashboard → Overview → Account ID |
| `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` | Pulumi state の R2 アクセス | R2 → Manage API Tokens |
| `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` | 同上 | 同上 |
| `PULUMI_CONFIG_PASSPHRASE` | State 暗号化 | 初回設定時に決めたもの |
| `SNOWFLAKE_ORGANIZATION_NAME` | Snowflake 接続 | Snowflake アカウント情報 |
| `SNOWFLAKE_ACCOUNT_NAME` | 同上 | 同上 |

**特に重要:** `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` には **D1:Edit, R2:Edit** の権限が必要です。R2 権限がないと `pulumi import` が 400 エラーになります（先ほどの原因はこれだった可能性が高い）。

`.env.local` を作って準備できたら教えてください。`pulumi import` → D1 修正 → PR 作成まで進めます。

1	# Cloudflare Data Infrastructure Project
2	
3	このドキュメントは、Cloudflareをベースとしたデータ基盤プロジェクトの開発ガイドです。
4	
5	## プロジェクト概要
6	
7	Cloudflareのエッジコンピューティングプラットフォームを活用し、グローバルに分散された低レイテンシ、高スケーラビリティなデータ基盤を構築するプロジェクトです。
8	
9	### 主な特徴
10	
11	- **エッジファースト**: Cloudflareのグローバルネットワークを活用した低レイテンシ処理
12	- **サーバーレス**: 自動スケーリングと運用負荷の削減
13	- **コスト最適化**: R2のエグレス無料など、従来のクラウドより低コスト
14	- **統合プラットフォーム**: Workers、KV、R2、D1など、統合されたサービス群
15	
16	## 技術スタック
17	
18	### コアテクノロジー
19	
20	- **Cloudflare Workers**: エッジコンピューティングプラットフォーム
21	- **TypeScript**: 型安全な開発（メイン言語）
22	- **Hono**: Workers用軽量Webフレームワーク
23	- **Python**: dbt変換パイプライン
24	- **Go**: Pulumiインフラ管理
25	- **Wrangler**: Cloudflare Workers用CLI
26	
27	### データストレージ
28	
29	| サービス | 用途 | 特徴 |
30	|---------|------|------|
31	| **Workers KV** | キー・バリューストア | 低レイテンシ読み取り、最終的整合性 |
32	| **R2** | オブジェクトストレージ | S3互換、エグレス無料 |
33	| **D1** | SQLデータベース | SQLite、リレーショナルデータ |
34	| **Analytics Engine** | 時系列データ | 高カーディナリティ、SQL分析 |
35	| **Durable Objects** | ステートフル処理 | 強整合性、WebSocket対応 |
36	| **Queues** | メッセージキュー | 非同期処理、リトライ機能 |
37	| **Hyperdrive** | DB接続プール | PostgreSQL高速接続 |
38	
39	## プロジェクト構造
40	
41	```
42	data-engineering-with-cloudflare/
43	├── .claude/                    # Claude Code設定
44	│   └── CLAUDE.md              # このファイル
45	├── .github/workflows/          # GitHub Actions CI/CD
46	├── ingestion/                  # データ取り込みWorker (TypeScript/Hono)
47	│   ├── src/
48	│   │   ├── index.ts           # メインエントリ
49	│   │   ├── types.ts           # 型定義
50	│   │   ├── services/          # サービス層
51	│   │   └── __tests__/         # テスト
52	│   ├── biome.json             # Biome設定
53	│   ├── wrangler.jsonc         # Wrangler設定
54	│   └── package.json           # pnpm依存関係
55	├── transform/
56	│   └── core/                  # dbtプロジェクト (DuckDB)
57	│       ├── models/            # dbtモデル
58	│       ├── macros/            # dbtマクロ
59	│       ├── tests/             # dbtテスト
60	│       ├── seeds/             # シードデータ
```

77	
78	## 開発ガイドライン
79	
80	### コーディング規約
81	
82	1. **TypeScript優先**: 型安全性を確保するため、TypeScriptを使用
83	2. **Biome**: Linting & Formattingに Biome を使用（ESLint/Prettier不使用）
84	3. **pnpm**: パッケージマネージャーにpnpmを使用
85	4. **エラーハンドリング**: すべての非同期処理で適切なエラーハンドリングを実装
86	5. **レスポンス時間**: Workers実行時間は50ms以内を目標（CPU time制限考慮）
87	6. **セキュリティ**: APIキーや認証情報は環境変数で管理、コードに埋め込まない
88	
89	### Workersベストプラクティス

610	
611	### インフラ開発サイクル
612	
613	#### Pulumiでのリソース管理
614	
615	```bash
616	# 1. インフラ変更
617	vim infrastructure/pulumi/main.go
618	
619	# 2. ローカルでpreview
620	cd infrastructure/pulumi
621	set -a; source .env.local; set +a
622	pulumi preview
623	
624	# 3. コミット＆PR作成
625	git add infrastructure/pulumi/
626	git commit -m "feat: Add R2 bucket for data lake"
627	git push
628	
629	# 4. PRでPreview確認
630	# → GitHub ActionsがPulumi Previewを実行
631	# → PRコメントにリソース変更が表示される
632	
633	# 5. マージ後、自動デプロイ
634	# → pulumi-deploy.yml が pulumi up --yes を実行
635	```
636	
637	#### D1マイグレーション
638	
639	```bash
640	# 1. マイグレーションファイル作成
641	vim infrastructure/d1/migrations/0002_add_new_table.sql
642	
643	# 2. ローカルで確認
644	cd infrastructure/d1
645	wrangler d1 migrations apply raw --local
646	
647	# 3. コミット＆PR作成
648	git add infrastructure/d1/migrations/
649	git commit -m "feat: Add new table for analytics"
650	git push
651	
652	# 4. マージ後、自動適用
653	# → d1-migrations.yml が wrangler d1 migrations apply raw を実行
654	```
655	
656	#### Workers デプロイ
657	
658	Workersは Cloudflare Dashboard の GitHub連携で自動デプロイされます:
659	
660	- **mainへのpush** → 本番デプロイ
661	- **PRの作成/更新** → プレビューURL発行
662	
663	手動デプロイ（必要な場合）:
664	```bash
665	cd ingestion
666	wrangler deploy
667	```
668	
669	### 依存関係のアップデート
670	
671	定期的に依存関係を更新します:
672	
673	```bash
674	# 1. 新ブランチ作成
675	git checkout -b chore/update-dependencies
676	
677	# 2. Wrangler更新
678	npm view wrangler version  # 最新版確認
679	# .github/workflows/d1-migrations.yml の wranglerVersion を更新
680	
681	# 3. Pulumi更新
682	cd infrastructure/pulumi
683	# go.mod の require セクションを更新
684	go mod tidy
685	
686	# 4. GitHub Actions のGoバージョン更新
687	# .github/workflows/pulumi-*.yml の go-version を更新
688	
689	# 5. コミット＆PR
690	git add .
691	git commit -m "chore: Update Wrangler and Pulumi dependencies"
692	git push
693	```
694	
695	### コミットメッセージ規約
696	
697	Conventional Commits形式:
698	
699	```text
700	<type>: <description>
701	
702	[optional body]
703	
704	[optional footer]
```

> DEVELOPER

Ingestion の続きをやる。何すればいい？

> AGENT

完了済みと残りを整理します。

### 完了済み
- streaming/ Worker コード（PR #59 マージ済み）
- Stream `linear_events` 作成済み（ID: `6592c325ce2a4bc388840c1ea1eedeeb`）
- R2 バケット `lake` 作成済み
- Data Catalog 有効化済み
- Pulumi 削除（PR #60 マージ済み）

### 残りの手順

| # | やること | 必要なもの |
|---|---------|-----------|
| 1 | **Sink 作成** | R2 Catalog Token（Dashboard で作成） |
| 2 | **Pipeline 作成** | Sink が先に必要 |
| 3 | **Worker デプロイ** | `cd streaming && pnpm deploy` |
| 4 | **シークレット設定** | `wrangler secret put LINEAR_WEBHOOK_SECRET` |
| 5 | **Linear Webhook 設定** | Linear Settings で URL + signing secret 設定 |
| 6 | **E2E 確認** | Issue 作成 → R2 SQL クエリ |

**最初のブロッカーは Step 1 の R2 Catalog Token です。**

Cloudflare Dashboard → My Profile → API Tokens → Create Token で、**R2 Admin Read & Write** 権限のトークンを作成してください。作成したらここに貼ってもらえれば（または環境変数にセットしてもらえれば）、残りは一気に実行できます。

> DEVELOPER

自分で実行したいのでコマンドを教えて

> AGENT

```bash
# === Step 1: Sink 作成 ===
# まず Cloudflare Dashboard で R2 Catalog Token を作成:
# My Profile → API Tokens → Create Token → R2 Admin Read & Write
# 作成したトークンを環境変数にセット:
export R2_CATALOG_TOKEN="your-token-here"

cd streaming
npx wrangler pipelines sinks create linear_events_sink \
  --type r2-data-catalog \
  --bucket lake \
  --namespace streaming \
  --table linear_events \
  --compression zstd \
  --roll-interval 300 \
  --catalog-token $R2_CATALOG_TOKEN

# === Step 2: Pipeline 作成 ===
npx wrangler pipelines create linear_events_pipeline \
  --sql-file sql/linear-pipeline.sql

# === Step 3: Worker デプロイ ===
pnpm deploy

# === Step 4: シークレット設定 ===
# プロンプトが出るので Linear の signing secret を入力
wrangler secret put LINEAR_WEBHOOK_SECRET

# === Step 5: Linear Webhook 設定 ===
# Linear Settings > Administration > API > Webhooks で:
#   URL: https://streaming.<your-subdomain>.workers.dev/webhooks/linear
#   Resource types: All
#   Signing secret をコピーして Step 4 で入力したものと同じ値を使う

# === Step 6: E2E 確認 ===
# 6a. ヘルスチェック
curl https://streaming.<your-subdomain>.workers.dev/health

# 6b. Linear で Issue を作成（手動で OK）

# 6c. 5分後（roll-interval）に R2 SQL でデータ確認
export WRANGLER_R2_SQL_AUTH_TOKEN=$R2_CATALOG_TOKEN
npx wrangler r2 sql query "lake" "
  SELECT action, type, created_at, data
  FROM streaming.linear_events
  LIMIT 5
"
```

> DEVELOPER

Pipeline 作成までやった。

> AGENT

残りは Step 3〜6 です。

```bash
# === Step 3: Worker デプロイ ===
cd streaming
pnpm deploy

# === Step 4: シークレット設定 ===
wrangler secret put LINEAR_WEBHOOK_SECRET
# → Linear Webhook の signing secret を入力

# === Step 5: Linear Webhook 設定 ===
# Linear Settings > Administration > API > Webhooks:
#   URL: https://streaming.<your-subdomain>.workers.dev/webhooks/linear
#   Resource types: All
#   Signing secret: Step 4 で入力したものと同じ値

# === Step 6: E2E 確認 ===
curl https://streaming.<your-subdomain>.workers.dev/health
# → {"status":"ok"} が返れば OK

# Linear で Issue を作成して 5分待ってから:
export WRANGLER_R2_SQL_AUTH_TOKEN=[REDACTED]"
npx wrangler r2 sql query "lake" "
  SELECT action, type, created_at, data
  FROM streaming.linear_events
  LIMIT 5
"
```

デプロイ URL は `pnpm deploy` の出力に表示されます。それを Linear Webhook の URL に設定してください。

<title></title>
<text>
## 2\. Bind an account secret to your Worker

[Bindings](/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/) allow your Worker to interact with resources on your Cloudflare account.

To bind an account secret to your Worker, you must have one of the following [roles within your Cloudflare account](/secrets-store/access-control/):

* Super Administrator
* Secrets Store Deployer

### Via Wrangler

1. Add a Secrets Store binding to your [Wrangler configuration file](/workers/wrangler/configuration/):  
   * `binding`: a descriptive name for your binding. This will be used in the Workers application when [accessing your secret on the env object](/secrets-store/integrations/workers/#3-access-the-secret-on-the-env-object).  
   * `store_id`: the corresponding Secrets Store ID where your account secret was created.  
   * `secret_name`: the unique secret name, defined when your account secret was created.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-6462)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-6463)

```

{

  "main": "./src/index.js",

  "secrets_store_secrets": [

    {

      "binding": "<BINDING_VARIABLE>",

      "store_id": "<STORE_ID>",

      "secret_name": "<MY_SECRET_NAME>"

    }

  ]

}


```

```

main = "./src/index.js"


[[secrets_store_secrets]]

binding = "<BINDING_VARIABLE>"

store_id = "<STORE_ID>"

secret_name = "<MY_SECRET_NAME>"


```

### Via Dashboard


</text>
</result>
<result>
<url>https://developers.cloudflare.com/https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/wrangler/configuration/</url>
<title></title>
<text>
local_protocol = "http"


```

## Secrets

[Secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/) are a type of binding that allow you to [attach encrypted text values](/workers/wrangler/commands/general/#secret) to your Worker.

### Local development

Warning

Do not use `vars` to store sensitive information in your Worker's Wrangler configuration file. Use secrets instead.

Put secrets for use in local development in either a `.dev.vars` file or a `.env` file, in the same directory as the Wrangler configuration file.

Note


### `secrets` configuration property

Note

This property is experimental and subject to change.

The `secrets` configuration property lets you declare the secret names your Worker requires in your Wrangler configuration file. Required secrets are validated during local development and deploy, and used as the source of truth for type generation.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-8407)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-8408)

```

{

  "secrets": {

    "required": ["API_KEY", "DB_PASSWORD"],

  },

}


```

```

[secrets]

required = [ "API_KEY", "DB_PASSWORD" ]


```

**Type generation**

When `secrets` is defined at any config level, `wrangler types` generates typed bindings from the names listed in `secrets.required` and no longer infers secret names from `.dev.vars` or `.env` files. This lets you run type generation in environments where those files are not present.

Per-environment secrets are supported. Each named environment produces its own interface, and the aggregated `Env` type marks secrets that only appear in some environments as optional.

**Deploy**

When `secrets` is defined, `wrangler deploy` and `wrangler versions upload` validate that all secrets in `secrets.required` are configured on the Worker before the operation succeeds. If any required secrets are missing, the command fails with an error listing which secrets need to be set.

## Module Aliasing

Note

If you're using the [Cloudflare Vite plugin](/workers/vite-plugin/), `alias` is replaced Vite's [resolve.alias ↗](https://vite.dev/config/shared-options.html#resolve-alias).


</text>

[Worker secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/) can be defined in a `.dev.vars.example` or `.env.example` file with a [dotenv ↗](https://www.npmjs.com/package/dotenv) format:

.dev.vars.example

```

COOKIE_SIGNING_KEY=my-secret # comment


```

[Secrets Store](/secrets-store/) secrets can be configured in the Wrangler configuration file as normal:

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-7453)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-7454)

```

{

  "name": "my-worker",

  "main": "./src/index.ts",

  // Set this to today's date

  "compatibility_date": "2026-03-31",

  "secrets_store_secrets": [

    {

      "binding": "API_KEY",

      "store_id": "demo",

      "secret_name": "api-key"

    }

  ]

}


```

```

name = "my-worker"

main = "./src/index.ts"

# Set this to today's date

compatibility_date = "2026-03-31"


[[secrets_store_secrets]]

binding = "API_KEY"

store_id = "demo"

secret_name = "api-key"


```

<title></title>
<text>
## Adding bindings

Next, we need to add bindings to access our secrets, KV values, and environment variables in Wrangler configuration.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-4059)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-4060)

JSONC

```

{

  "name": "my-container-worker",

  "vars": {

    "ENV_VAR": "my-env-var"

  },

  "secrets_store_secrets": [

    {

      "binding": "SECRET_STORE",

      "store_id": "demo",

      "secret_name": "SECRET_STORE_SECRET"

    }

  ],

  "kv_namespaces": [

    {

      "binding": "DEMO_KV",

      "id": "<your-kv-namespace-id>"

    }

  ]

  // rest of the configuration...

}


```

TOML

```

name = "my-container-worker"


[vars]

ENV_VAR = "my-env-var"


[[secrets_store_secrets]]

binding = "SECRET_STORE"

store_id = "demo"

secret_name = "SECRET_STORE_SECRET"


[[kv_namespaces]]

binding = "DEMO_KV"

id = "<your-kv-namespace-id>"


```

Note that `"WORKER_SECRET"` does not need to be specified in the Wrangler config file, as it is automatically added to `env`.

Also note that we did not configure anything specific for environment variables, secrets, or KV values in the _container-related_ portion of the Wrangler configuration file.

## Using `envVars` on the Container class

Now, let's pass the env vars and secrets to our container using the `envVars` field in the `Container` class:

JavaScript

```

// https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/#importing-env-as-a-global

<title></title>
<text>
## Secrets

Secrets are a type of binding that allow you to attach encrypted text values to your Pages Function. You cannot see secrets after you set them and can only access secrets programmatically on `context.env`. Secrets are used for storing sensitive information like API keys and auth tokens.

To add secrets to your Pages project:

1. In the Cloudflare dashboard, go to the **Workers & Pages** page.  
[ Go to **Workers & Pages** ](https://dash.cloudflare.com/?to=/:account/workers-and-pages)
2. Select your Pages project.
3. Go to **Settings** \> **Variables and Secrets** \> **Add**.
4. Set a variable name and value.
5. Select **Encrypt** to create your secret.
6. Select **Save**.

You use secrets the same way as environment variables. When setting secrets with Wrangler or in the Cloudflare dashboard, it needs to be done before a deployment that uses those secrets. For more guidance, refer to [Environment variables](#environment-variables).

### Local development with secrets

Warning

Do not use `vars` to store sensitive information in your Worker's Wrangler configuration file. Use secrets instead.


</text>
</result>
<result>
<url>https://developers.cloudflare.com/https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/wrangler/environments/</url>
<title></title>
<text>
[env.staging.service]

binding = "<BINDING_NAME>"

service = "worker-a-staging"


```

Explain Code

### Secrets for production

You may assign environment-specific [secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/) by running the command [wrangler secret put <KEY> -env](/workers/wrangler/commands/general/#secret-put). You can also create `dotenv` type files named `.dev.vars.<environment-name>`.

Like other environment variables, secrets are [non-inheritable](/workers/wrangler/configuration/#non-inheritable-keys) and must be defined per environment.

### Secrets in local development

Warning

Do not use `vars` to store sensitive information in your Worker's Wrangler configuration file. Use secrets instead.


</text>

<title></title>
<text>
## Configuration

### Wrangler bindings

Add a `worker_loaders` binding to your `wrangler.jsonc`. This is the only binding required:

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-2270)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-2271)

```

{

  "$schema": "./node_modules/wrangler/config-schema.json",

  "worker_loaders": [

    {

      "binding": "LOADER"

    }

  ],

  "compatibility_flags": [

    "nodejs_compat"

  ]

}


```

```

worker_loaders = [{ binding = "LOADER" }]

compatibility_flags = ["nodejs_compat"]


```

## How it works

## Deploy to Cloudflare buttons now support Worker environment variables, secrets, and Secrets Store secrets

Jul 29, 2025 

[ Workers ](/workers/)[ Secrets Store ](/secrets-store/) 

Any template which uses [Worker environment variables](/workers/configuration/environment-variables/), [secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/), or [Secrets Store secrets](/secrets-store/) can now be deployed using a [Deploy to Cloudflare button](/workers/platform/deploy-buttons/).

Define environment variables and secrets store bindings in your Wrangler configuration file as normal:

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-112)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-113)

JSONC

```

{

  "name": "my-worker",

  "main": "./src/index.ts",

  // Set this to today's date

  "compatibility_date": "2026-04-12",

  "vars": {

    "API_HOST": "https://example.com",

  },

  "secrets_store_secrets": [

    {

      "binding": "API_KEY",

      "store_id": "demo",

      "secret_name": "api-key"

    }

  ]

}


```

Explain Code

TOML

```

name = "my-worker"

main = "./src/index.ts"

# Set this to today's date

compatibility_date = "2026-04-12"


[vars]

API_HOST = "https://example.com"


[[secrets_store_secrets]]

binding = "API_KEY"

store_id = "demo"

secret_name = "api-key"


```

Explain Code

Add secrets to a `.dev.vars.example` or `.env.example` file:

.dev.vars.example

```


</text>

<url>https://developers.cloudflare.com/https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/static-assets/migration-guides/migrate-from-pages/</url>

<text>
# Set this to today's date

compatibility_date = "2026-04-02"

compatibility_flags = [ "nodejs_compat" ]

main = "./worker/index.ts"


[placement]

mode = "smart"


[assets]

directory = "./dist/client/"

binding = "ASSETS"


```

### Variables, secrets and bindings

[Variables](/workers/configuration/environment-variables/) and [bindings](/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/) can be set in your [Wrangler configuration file](/workers/wrangler/configuration/) and are made available in your Worker's environment (`env`). [Secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/) can uploaded with Wrangler or defined in the Cloudflare dashboard for [production](/workers/configuration/secrets/#adding-secrets-to-your-project) and [.dev.vars for local development](/workers/configuration/secrets/#local-development-with-secrets).

If you are [using Workers Builds](#builds), ensure you also [configure any variables relevant to the build environment there](/workers/ci-cd/builds/configuration/). Unlike Pages, Workers does not share the same set of runtime and build-time variables.

### Wrangler commands

Experimental: Enable automatic resource provisioning
* `--experimental-auto-create` ` boolean ` alias: --x-auto-create default: true  
Automatically provision draft bindings with new resources

# How to

Refer to the sections below to learn about common actions you might want to take when managing your data in Secrets Store.

You must have a [Super Administrator or Secrets Store Admin role](/secrets-store/access-control/) within your Cloudflare account.

## Manage via Wrangler

[Wrangler](/workers/wrangler/) is a command-line interface (CLI) that allows you to manage [Cloudflare Workers](/workers/) projects. Refer to [Wrangler commands](/workers/wrangler/commands/secrets-store/#secrets-store-secret) for guidance on how to use it with Secrets Store.

## Create a secret

* [ API ](#tab-panel-6474)

<text>
## 1\. Set up account secrets in Secrets Store

Follow the steps below to create secrets. You must have a [Super Administrator or a Secrets Store Admin role](/secrets-store/access-control/) within your Cloudflare account.

Note

You may also add account secrets directly from the Workers settings on the dashboard. You can skip to [step 2](#via-dashboard) to do that.

* [ Wrangler ](#tab-panel-6464)
* [ Dashboard ](#tab-panel-6465)
* [ API ](#tab-panel-6466)

Use the [Wrangler command](/workers/wrangler/commands/secrets-store/#secrets-store-secret) `secrets-store secret create`.

To use the following example, replace the store ID and secret name by your actual data. You can find and copy the store ID from the [Secrets Store tab ↗](https://dash.cloudflare.com/?to=/:account/secrets-store/) on the dashboard or use `wrangler secrets-store store list`.

Note that a secret name cannot contain spaces.

Terminal window

```

npx wrangler secrets-store secret create <STORE_ID> --name MY_SECRET_NAME --scopes workers --remote


```

```

✓ Enter a secret value: › ***


🔐 Creating secret... (Name: MY_SECRET_NAME, Value: REDACTED, Scopes: workers, Comment: undefined)

✓ Select an account: › My account

✅ Created secret! (ID: 13bc7498c6374a4e9d13be091c3c65f1)


```


# Workers integration

[Cloudflare Secrets Store](/secrets-store/) is a secure, centralized location in which account-level secrets are stored and managed. The secrets are securely encrypted and stored across all Cloudflare data centers.

Consider the steps below to learn how to use values from your account secrets store with [Cloudflare Workers](/workers/).

Note

This is different from Workers [Variables and Secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/), where you define and manage your secrets on a per-Worker level.

## Before you begin

* If [using the Dashboard](#via-dashboard), make sure you already have a Workers application. Refer to the [Workers get started](/workers/get-started/dashboard/) for guidance.
* You should also have a store created under the **Secrets Store** tab on the Dashboard. The first store in your account is created automatically when a user with [Super Administrator or Secrets Store Admin role](/secrets-store/access-control/) interacts with it.  
   * If no store exists in your account yet and you have the necessary permissions, you can use the [Wrangler command](/workers/wrangler/commands/secrets-store/#secrets-store-store) `secrets-store store create <name> --remote` to create your first store.

Local development mode

This guide assumes you are working in production. To use Secrets Store locally, you must use `secrets-store secret` [Wrangler commands](/workers/wrangler/commands/) without the `--remote` flag.

## 1\. Set up account secrets in Secrets Store

Follow the steps below to create secrets. You must have a [Super Administrator or a Secrets Store Admin role](/secrets-store/access-control/) within your Cloudflare account.

Note


</text>
</result>

> AGENT

手順をまとめます。

```bash
# === Step 1: Store を確認（または作成） ===
npx wrangler secrets-store store list --remote
# Store がなければ作成:
# npx wrangler secrets-store store create default --remote

# === Step 2: Secret を作成 ===
# <STORE_ID> は Step 1 で確認した ID に置き換え
npx wrangler secrets-store secret create <STORE_ID> \
  --name LINEAR_WEBHOOK_SECRET \
  --scopes workers \
  --remote
# → プロンプトで signing secret の値を入力

# === Step 3: wrangler.jsonc を更新 ===
# "pipelines" の後に secrets_store_secrets を追加して再デプロイが必要
# <STORE_ID> を実際の値に置き換えてください
```

`wrangler.jsonc` の変更内容：

```jsonc
{
  // ...既存の設定...
  "pipelines": [
    {
      "pipeline": "6592c325ce2a4bc388840c1ea1eedeeb",
      "binding": "STREAM"
    }
  ],
  "secrets_store_secrets": [
    {
      "binding": "LINEAR_WEBHOOK_SECRET",
      "store_id": "<STORE_ID>",
      "secret_name": "LINEAR_WEBHOOK_SECRET"
    }
  ]
}
```

```bash
# === Step 4: 再デプロイ ===
cd streaming
pnpm run deploy
```

Webhook は届いていますが **401 を返しています**。署名検証に失敗しています。

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
`POST /webhooks/linear` → status **401**。Webhook は到達しているが、HMAC 署名検証で弾かれている。Secrets Store のバインディングが `string` ではなく別の型で返っている可能性がある。
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Secrets Store のバインディングは従来の `wrangler secret` と異なり、`.get()` メソッドで値を取得する必要がある可能性があります。確認します。

---
title: Secrets
description: Store sensitive information, like API keys and auth tokens, in your Worker.

Copy page

# Secrets

## Background

Secrets are a type of binding that allow you to attach encrypted text values to your Worker. Secrets are used for storing sensitive information like API keys and auth tokens.

You can access secrets in your Worker code through:

* The [env parameter](/workers/runtime-apis/handlers/fetch/#parameters) passed to your Worker's [fetch event handler](/workers/runtime-apis/handlers/fetch/).
* Importing `env` from [cloudflare:workers](/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/#importing-env-as-a-global) to access secrets from anywhere in your code.
* [process.env](/workers/configuration/environment-variables) in Workers that have [Node.js compatibility](/workers/runtime-apis/nodejs/) enabled.

## Access your secrets with Workers

Secrets can be accessed from Workers as you would any other [environment variables](/workers/configuration/environment-variables/). For instance, given a `DB_CONNECTION_STRING` secret, you can access it in your Worker code through the `env` parameter:

index.js

```


## Access your secrets with Workers

Secrets can be accessed from Workers as you would any other [environment variables](/workers/configuration/environment-variables/). For instance, given a `DB_CONNECTION_STRING` secret, you can access it in your Worker code through the `env` parameter:

index.js

```

import postgres from "postgres";


export default {

  async fetch(request, env, ctx) {

    const sql = postgres(env.DB_CONNECTION_STRING);


    const result = await sql`SELECT * FROM products;`;


    return new Response(JSON.stringify(result), {

      headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },

    });

  },

};


```

You can also import `env` from `cloudflare:workers` to access secrets from anywhere in your code, including outside of request handlers:

* [  JavaScript ](#tab-panel-7068)
* [  TypeScript ](#tab-panel-7069)

JavaScript

```

import { env } from "cloudflare:workers";

import postgres from "postgres";


// Initialize the database client at the top level using a secret

const sql = postgres(env.DB_CONNECTION_STRING);


export default {

  async fetch(request) {

    const result = await sql`SELECT * FROM products;`;


    return new Response(JSON.stringify(result), {

      headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },

    });

  },

};


```

TypeScript

```

import { env } from "cloudflare:workers";

import postgres from "postgres";


// Initialize the database client at the top level using a secret

const sql = postgres(env.DB_CONNECTION_STRING);


export default {

  async fetch(request: Request): Promise<Response> {

    const result = await sql`SELECT * FROM products;`;


    return new Response(JSON.stringify(result), {

      headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },

    });

  },

};


```

For more details on accessing `env` globally, refer to [Importing env as a global](/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/#importing-env-as-a-global).

Secrets Store (beta)


## Compare secrets and environment variables

Use secrets for sensitive information

Do not use plaintext environment variables to store sensitive information. Use [secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/) or [Secrets Store bindings](/secrets-store/integrations/workers/) instead.

[Secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/) are [environment variables](/workers/configuration/environment-variables/). The difference is secret values are not visible within Wrangler or Cloudflare dashboard after you define them. This means that sensitive data, including passwords or API tokens, should always be encrypted to prevent data leaks. To your Worker, there is no difference between an environment variable and a secret. The secret's value is passed through as defined.

## Related resources

<title></title>
<text>
### `secrets-store secret get`

Get a secret within a store

* [  npm ](#tab-panel-8216)
* [  pnpm ](#tab-panel-8217)
* [  yarn ](#tab-panel-8218)

Terminal window

```

npx wrangler secrets-store secret get [STORE-ID]


```

Terminal window

```

pnpm wrangler secrets-store secret get [STORE-ID]


```

Terminal window

```

yarn wrangler secrets-store secret get [STORE-ID]


```

* `[STORE-ID]` ` string ` required  
ID of the store in which the secret resides
* `--secret-id` ` string ` required  
ID of the secret to retrieve
* `--remote` ` boolean ` default: false  
Execute command against remote Secrets Store
* `--persist-to` ` string `  
Directory for local persistence

Global flags

* `--v` ` boolean ` alias: --version  
Show version number
* `--cwd` ` string `  
Run as if Wrangler was started in the specified directory instead of the current working directory
* `--config` ` string ` alias: --c  
Path to Wrangler configuration file
* `--env` ` string ` alias: --e  
Environment to use for operations, and for selecting .env and .dev.vars files
* `--env-file` ` string `  
Path to an .env file to load - can be specified multiple times - values from earlier files are overridden by values in later files
* `--experimental-provision` ` boolean ` aliases: --x-provision default: true  
Experimental: Enable automatic resource provisioning
* `--experimental-auto-create` ` boolean ` alias: --x-auto-create default: true  
Automatically provision draft bindings with new resources

The following is an example with the expected output:

Terminal window

```

npx wrangler secrets-store secret get 8f7a1cdced6342c18d223ece462fd88d --secret-id 13bc7498c6374a4e9d13be091c3c65f1 --remote


```

```

🔐 Getting secret... (ID: 13bc7498c6374a4e9d13be091c3c65f1)

✓ Select an account: › My account

| Name                        | ID                                  | StoreID                             | Comment | Scopes  | Status  | Created                | Modified               |


</text>

<title></title>
<text>
### Via Dashboard

1. In the Cloudflare dashboard, go to **Workers & Pages**.  
[ Go to **Workers & Pages** ](https://dash.cloudflare.com/?to=/:account/workers-and-pages)
2. Select a Workers application.
3. Go to **Settings** \> **Bindings** and select **Add**.
4. On the **Add a resource binding** side panel, choose **Secrets Store**.
5. Fill in the required fields:  
   * **Variable name**: a name for the binding. This will be used for your Worker to access the secret ([step 3](#3-access-the-secret-on-the-env-object) below).  
   * **Secret name**: select from the list of available account secrets created in [step 1](#1-set-up-account-secrets-in-secrets-store).  
   * (Optional - Admins only) If the secret you need does not exist yet, select **Create secret**. This will add an account level secret in the same way as if you had [created it on the Secrets Store](/secrets-store/manage-secrets/).
6. Select **Deploy** to deploy your binding. When deploying, there are two options:  
   * **Deploy:** Immediately deploy the binding to 100% of your audience.  
   * **Save version:** Save a version of the binding which you can deploy in the future.

## 3\. Access the secret on the `env` object

[Bindings](/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/) are located on the `env` object. To access the secret you first need an asynchronous call.

### Call `get()` on the binding variable

Local development mode


## 2\. Bind an account secret to your Worker

[Bindings](/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/) allow your Worker to interact with resources on your Cloudflare account.

To bind an account secret to your Worker, you must have one of the following [roles within your Cloudflare account](/secrets-store/access-control/):

* Super Administrator
* Secrets Store Deployer

### Via Wrangler

1. Add a Secrets Store binding to your [Wrangler configuration file](/workers/wrangler/configuration/):  
   * `binding`: a descriptive name for your binding. This will be used in the Workers application when [accessing your secret on the env object](/secrets-store/integrations/workers/#3-access-the-secret-on-the-env-object).  
   * `store_id`: the corresponding Secrets Store ID where your account secret was created.  
   * `secret_name`: the unique secret name, defined when your account secret was created.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-6462)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-6463)

```

{

  "main": "./src/index.js",

  "secrets_store_secrets": [

    {

      "binding": "<BINDING_VARIABLE>",

      "store_id": "<STORE_ID>",

      "secret_name": "<MY_SECRET_NAME>"

    }

  ]

}


```

```

main = "./src/index.js"


[[secrets_store_secrets]]

binding = "<BINDING_VARIABLE>"

store_id = "<STORE_ID>"

secret_name = "<MY_SECRET_NAME>"


```

### Via Dashboard


## 3\. Access the secret on the `env` object

[Bindings](/workers/runtime-apis/bindings/) are located on the `env` object. To access the secret you first need an asynchronous call.

### Call `get()` on the binding variable

Local development mode

You cannot access production secrets (created on the dashboard, via API, or with the `--remote` flag) from your local development setup. To use Secrets Store locally, you must use `secrets-store secret` [Wrangler commands](/workers/wrangler/commands/) without the `--remote` flag.

JavaScript

```

export default {

  async fetch(request, env) {

    // Example of using the secret safely in an API request

    const APIkey = await env.<BINDING_VARIABLE>.get()


    const response = await fetch("https://api.example.com/data", {

      headers: { "Authorization": `Bearer ${APIKey}` },

    });


    if (!response.ok) {

      return new Response("Failed to fetch data", { status: response.status });

    }


    const data = await response.json();

    return new Response(JSON.stringify(data), {

      headers: { "Content-Type": "application/json" },

    });

  },

};


```

```json

```

# Env Vars and Secrets

**Last reviewed:**  10 months ago 

Pass in environment variables and secrets to your container

Environment variables can be passed into a Container using the `envVars` field in the [Container](/containers/container-class/) class, or by setting manually when the Container starts.

Secrets can be passed into a Container by using [Worker Secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/)or the [Secret Store](/secrets-store/integrations/workers/), then passing them into the Container as environment variables.

KV values can be passed into a Container by using [Workers KV](/kv/), then reading the values and passing them into the Container as environment variables.

These examples show the various ways to pass in secrets, KV values, and environment variables. In each, we will be passing in:

* the variable `"ENV_VAR"` as a hard-coded environment variable
* the secret `"WORKER_SECRET"` as a secret from Worker Secrets
* the secret `"SECRET_STORE_SECRET"` as a secret from the Secret Store
* the value `"KV_VALUE"` as a value from Workers KV

In practice, you may just use one of the methods for storing secrets and data, but we will show all methods for completeness.

## Creating secrets and KV data

First, let's create the `"WORKER_SECRET"` secret in Worker Secrets:

 npm  yarn  pnpm 

```
npx wrangler secret put WORKER_SECRET
```

```
yarn wrangler secret put WORKER_SECRET
```

```
pnpm wrangler secret put WORKER_SECRET
```


## Creating secrets and KV data

First, let's create the `"WORKER_SECRET"` secret in Worker Secrets:

 npm  yarn  pnpm 

```
npx wrangler secret put WORKER_SECRET
```

```
yarn wrangler secret put WORKER_SECRET
```

```
pnpm wrangler secret put WORKER_SECRET
```

Then, let's create a store called "demo" in the Secret Store, and add the `"SECRET_STORE_SECRET"` secret to it:

 npm  yarn  pnpm 

```
npx wrangler secrets-store store create demo --remote
```

```
yarn wrangler secrets-store store create demo --remote
```

```
pnpm wrangler secrets-store store create demo --remote
```

 npm  yarn  pnpm 

```
npx wrangler secrets-store secret create demo --name SECRET_STORE_SECRET --scopes workers --remote
```

```
yarn wrangler secrets-store secret create demo --name SECRET_STORE_SECRET --scopes workers --remote
```

```
pnpm wrangler secrets-store secret create demo --name SECRET_STORE_SECRET --scopes workers --remote
```

Next, let's create a KV namespace called `DEMO_KV` and add a key-value pair:

 npm  yarn  pnpm 

```
npx wrangler kv namespace create DEMO_KV
```

```
yarn wrangler kv namespace create DEMO_KV
```

```
pnpm wrangler kv namespace create DEMO_KV
```

 npm  yarn  pnpm 

```
npx wrangler kv key put --binding DEMO_KV KV_VALUE 'Hello from KV!'
```

```
yarn wrangler kv key put --binding DEMO_KV KV_VALUE 'Hello from KV!'
```

```
pnpm wrangler kv key put --binding DEMO_KV KV_VALUE 'Hello from KV!'
```

For full details on how to create secrets, see the [Workers Secrets documentation](/workers/configuration/secrets/)and the [Secret Store documentation](/secrets-store/integrations/workers/). For KV setup, see the [Workers KV documentation](/kv/).

## Adding bindings

Next, we need to add bindings to access our secrets, KV values, and environment variables in Wrangler configuration.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-4059)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-4060)

JSONC

```

{

  "name": "my-container-worker",

  "vars": {


</text>

## Compare secrets and environment variables

Use secrets for sensitive information

Do not use plaintext environment variables to store sensitive information. Use [secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/) or [Secrets Store bindings](/secrets-store/integrations/workers/) instead.

[Secrets](/workers/configuration/secrets/) are [environment variables](/workers/configuration/environment-variables/). The difference is secret values are not visible within Wrangler or Cloudflare dashboard after you define them. This means that sensitive data, including passwords or API tokens, should always be encrypted to prevent data leaks. To your Worker, there is no difference between an environment variable and a secret. The secret's value is passed through as defined.

### Local development with secrets

Warning

Do not use `vars` to store sensitive information in your Worker's Wrangler configuration file. Use secrets instead.

```javascript
// ❌ 現在のコード（string として直接参照）
c.env.LINEAR_WEBHOOK_SECRET

// ✅ 正しい使い方
await c.env.LINEAR_WEBHOOK_SECRET.get()
```

R2 に書き込まれるまで roll-interval の 300秒（5分）待つ必要があります。その間にコード変更をコミットして PR にしておきましょうか？

> DEVELOPER

roll-interval って何？

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**roll-interval** は Pipelines の Sink がデータを Iceberg テーブル（Parquet ファイル）に書き出す間隔です。

- Webhook イベントは受信直後に Stream のバッファに入る
- Sink は **300秒（5分）ごと**にバッファに溜まったデータをまとめて1つの Parquet ファイルとして R2 に書き出す
- つまり R2 SQL でクエリできるようになるまで最大5分のラグがある

値を小さくすれば（例: 30秒）レイテンシは下がりますが、ファイルが細かくなりすぎてクエリ性能が悪化します。Linear Webhook の頻度（数十件/時間程度）では 300秒がちょうどいいバランスです。
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

5分後に R2 SQL でデータが見えるか確認しますか？

> DEVELOPER

<result>
<url>https://developers.cloudflare.com/https://developers.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/usage/observability/</url>
<title></title>
<text>
## Capture logs with Tail Workers

To save logs emitted by a Dynamic Worker, you need to capture them and write them somewhere they can be stored. Setting this up requires three steps:

1. Enabling [Workers Logs](/workers/observability/logs/workers-logs/) on the loader Worker so that log output is saved.
2. Defining a Tail Worker that receives logs from the Dynamic Worker and writes them to Workers Logs.
3. Attaching the Tail Worker to the Dynamic Worker when you create it.

Note

Tail Workers run asynchronously after the Dynamic Worker has already sent its response, so they do not add latency to the request.

### Enable Workers Logs on the loader Worker

Enable [Workers Logs](/workers/observability/logs/workers-logs/) by adding the `observability` setting to the loader Worker's Wrangler configuration. However, Workers Logs only captures log output from the loader Worker itself. Dynamic Workers are separate, so their `console.log()` calls are not included automatically. To get Dynamic Worker logs into Workers Logs, you need to define a Tail Worker that receives logs from the Dynamic Worker and writes them into the loader Worker's Workers Logs.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-4548)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-4549)

```

{

  "$schema": "./node_modules/wrangler/config-schema.json",

  "observability": {

    "enabled": true,

    "head_sampling_rate": 1

  }

}


```

```

[observability]

enabled = true

head_sampling_rate = 1


```

### Define the Tail Worker

When a Dynamic Worker runs, the runtime collects all of its `console.log()` calls, exceptions, and request metadata. By default, those logs are discarded after the Dynamic Worker finishes.


</text>
</result>
<result>
<url>https://developers.cloudflare.com/https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/observability/logs/workers-logs/</url>
<title></title>
<text>
## Enable Workers Logs

Wrangler version

Minimum required Wrangler version: 3.78.6\. Check your version by running `wrangler --version`. To update Wrangler, refer to [Install/Update Wrangler](/workers/wrangler/install-and-update/).

You must add the observability setting for your Worker to write logs to Workers Logs. Add the following setting to your Worker's Wrangler file and redeploy your Worker.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-7429)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-7430)

```

{

  "observability": {

    "enabled": true,

    "head_sampling_rate": 1 // optional. default = 1.

  }

}


```

```

[observability]

enabled = true

head_sampling_rate = 1


```

[Head-based sampling](/workers/observability/logs/workers-logs/#head-based-sampling) allows you set the percentage of Workers requests that are logged.

### Enabling with environments

[Environments](/workers/wrangler/environments/) allow you to deploy the same Worker application with different configurations. For example, you may want to configure a different `head_sampling_rate` to staging and production. To configure observability for an environment named `staging`: 1\. Add the following configuration below `[env.staging]`

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-7433)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-7434)

```

{

  "env": {

    "staging": {

      "observability": {

        "enabled": true,

        "head_sampling_rate": 1 // optional

      }

    }

  }

}


```

```

[env.staging.observability]

enabled = true

head_sampling_rate = 1


```

1. Deploy your Worker with `npx wrangler deploy -e staging`
2. Repeat step 1 and 2 for each environment.

## View logs from the dashboard

Access logs for your Worker from the Cloudflare dashboard:


</text>
</result>
<result>
<url>https://developers.cloudflare.com/https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/observability/exporting-opentelemetry-data/</url>
<title></title>
<text>
## Enabling OpenTelemetry export for your Worker

After setting up destinations in the dashboard, configure your Worker to export telemetry data by updating your Wrangler configuration. Your destination name configured in your configuration file should be the same as the destination configured in the dashboard.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-7472)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-7473)

JSONC

```

{

  "observability": {

    "traces": {

      "enabled": true,

      "destinations": ["tracing-destination-name"],


      // traces sample rate of 5%

      "head_sampling_rate": 0.05,


      // (optional) set to false to only export traces to your

      // destination without persisting them in the Cloudflare dashboard

      "persist": false

    },

    "logs": {

      "enabled": true,

      "destinations": ["logs-destination-name"],

      // logs sample rate of 60%

      "head_sampling_rate": 0.6,


      // (optional) set to false to only export logs to your

      // destination without persisting them in the Cloudflare dashboard

      "persist": false

    }

  }

}


```

TOML

```

[observability.traces]

enabled = true

destinations = [ "tracing-destination-name" ]

head_sampling_rate = 0.05

persist = false


[observability.logs]

enabled = true

destinations = [ "logs-destination-name" ]

head_sampling_rate = 0.6

persist = false


```

`persist` and pricing

By default, `persist` is `true`, which means logs and traces are both exported to your destination and stored in the Cloudflare dashboard. Dashboard storage is billed [separately](/workers/observability/logs/workers-logs/#pricing). Set `persist` to `false` if you only need data in your external destination.

Once you've configured your Wrangler configuration file, redeploy your Worker for new configurations to take effect. Note that it may take a few minutes for events to reach your destination.

## Destination status


</text>

<title></title>
<text>
#### Workers Logs is now Generally Available  
[Workers Logs](/workers/observability/logs/workers-logs) is now Generally Available. With a [small change](/workers/observability/logs/workers-logs/#enable-workers-logs) to your Wrangler configuration, Workers Logs ingests, indexes, and stores all logs emitted from your Workers for up to 7 days.  
We've introduced a number of changes during our beta period, including:  
   * Dashboard enhancements with customizable fields as columns in the Logs view and support for invocation-based grouping  
   * Performance improvements to ensure no adverse impact  
   * Public [API endpoints ↗](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/workers/subresources/observability/) for broader consumption  
The API documents three endpoints: list the keys in the telemetry dataset, run a query, and list the unique values for a key. For more, visit our [REST API documentation ↗](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/workers/subresources/observability/).  
Visit the [docs](/workers/observability/query-builder) to learn more about the capabilities and methods exposed by the Query Builder. Start using Workers Logs and the Query Builder today by enabling observability for your Workers:  
   * [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-1582)  
   * [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-1583)  
JSONC  
```  
{  
  "observability": {  
    "enabled": true,  
    "logs": {  
      "invocation_logs": true,  
      "head_sampling_rate": 1 // optional. default = 1.  
    }  
  }  
}  
```  
TOML  
```  
[observability]  
enabled = true  
  [observability.logs]  
  invocation_logs = true  
  head_sampling_rate = 1  
```


</text>
</result>

#### Workers Logs is now Generally Available

[Workers Logs](/workers/observability/logs/workers-logs) is now Generally Available. With a [small change](/workers/observability/logs/workers-logs/#enable-workers-logs) to your Wrangler configuration, Workers Logs ingests, indexes, and stores all logs emitted from your Workers for up to 7 days.

The API documents three endpoints: list the keys in the telemetry dataset, run a query, and list the unique values for a key. For more, visit our [REST API documentation ↗](https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/workers/subresources/observability/).

Visit the [docs](/workers/observability/query-builder) to learn more about the capabilities and methods exposed by the Query Builder. Start using Workers Logs and the Query Builder today by enabling observability for your Workers:

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-134)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-135)

JSONC

```

{

  "observability": {

    "enabled": true,

    "logs": {

      "invocation_logs": true,

      "head_sampling_rate": 1 // optional. default = 1.

    }

<title></title>
<text>
# Set this to today's date

compatibility_date = "2026-04-08"

compatibility_flags = [ "nodejs_compat" ]


[assets]

directory = "./dist"


```

For more information, refer to [Workers Static Assets](/workers/static-assets/).

## Observability

### Enable Workers Logs and Traces

Production Workers without observability are a black box. Enable logs and traces before you deploy to production. When an intermittent error appears, you need data already being collected to diagnose it.

Enable them in your Wrangler configuration and use `head_sampling_rate` to control volume and manage costs. A sampling rate of `1` captures everything; lower it for high-traffic Workers.

Use structured JSON logging with `console.log` so logs are searchable and filterable. Use `console.error` for errors and `console.warn` for warnings. These appear at the correct severity level in the Workers Observability dashboard.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-7028)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-7029)

JSONC

```

{

  "name": "my-worker",

  "main": "src/index.ts",

  // Set this to today's date

  "compatibility_date": "2026-04-08",

  "compatibility_flags": ["nodejs_compat"],


  "observability": {

    "enabled": true,

    "logs": {

      // Capture 100% of logs — lower this for high-traffic Workers

      "head_sampling_rate": 1,

    },

    "traces": {

      "enabled": true,

      "head_sampling_rate": 0.01, // Sample 1% of traces

    },

  },

}


```

TOML

```

name = "my-worker"

main = "src/index.ts"

# Set this to today's date

compatibility_date = "2026-04-08"

compatibility_flags = [ "nodejs_compat" ]


[observability]

enabled = true


  [observability.logs]

  head_sampling_rate = 1


  [observability.traces]

  enabled = true

  head_sampling_rate = 0.01


```


</text>
</result>

<title></title>
<text>
## Step 3: Configure your Worker

With your destinations created in the Cloudflare dashboard, update your Worker's configuration to enable telemetry export.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-7419)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-7420)

```

{

  "observability": {

    "traces": {

      "enabled": true,

      // Must match the destination name in the dashboard

      "destinations": ["honeycomb-traces"]

    },

    "logs": {

      "enabled": true,

      // Must match the destination name in the dashboard

      "destinations": ["honeycomb-logs"]

    }

  }

}


```

```

[observability.traces]

enabled = true

destinations = [ "honeycomb-traces" ]


[observability.logs]

enabled = true

destinations = [ "honeycomb-logs" ]


```

After updating your configuration, deploy your Worker for the changes to take effect.

Note

It may take a few minutes after deployment for data to appear in Honeycomb.

## Step 4: Configure your Worker

With your destinations created in the Cloudflare dashboard, update your Worker's configuration to enable telemetry export.

* [  wrangler.jsonc ](#tab-panel-7421)
* [  wrangler.toml ](#tab-panel-7422)

```

{

  "observability": {

    "traces": {

      "enabled": true,

      // Must match the destination name in the dashboard

      "destinations": ["sentry-traces"]

    },

    "logs": {

      "enabled": true,

      // Must match the destination name in the dashboard

      "destinations": ["sentry-logs"]

    }

  }

}


```

```

[observability.traces]

enabled = true

destinations = [ "sentry-traces" ]


[observability.logs]

enabled = true

destinations = [ "sentry-logs" ]


```

After updating your configuration, deploy your Worker for the changes to take effect.

Note

It may take a few minutes after deployment for data to appear in Sentry.

> AGENT

Tail Workers は古い仕組みで、現在は **Workers Observability（Workers Logs）** が推奨です。streaming Worker は既に有効化済みです。

Dashboard にその表示が出ているのは、Tail Workers の接続が**未設定**というだけで、問題ありません。先ほど Workers Observability で 200 レスポンスのログを確認できていた通り、ログは正常に収集されています。

特に追加設定は不要です。R2 SQL でデータが書き込まれたか確認しますか？そろそろ 5分経つ頃です。

1	import { Hono } from "hono";
2	import { verifySignature } from "../lib/signature";
3	import type { Env, LinearStreamEvent, LinearWebhookPayload } from "../types";
4	
5	const TIMESTAMP_TOLERANCE_MS = 60_000;
6	
7	/**
8	 * Linear Webhook ペイロードを Stream スキーマに正規化する
9	 */
10	export function normalizePayload(payload: LinearWebhookPayload): LinearStreamEvent {
11	  return {
12	    action: payload.action,
13	    type: payload.type,
14	    created_at: payload.createdAt,
15	    webhook_id: payload.webhookId,
16	    webhook_timestamp: payload.webhookTimestamp,
17	    organization_id: payload.organizationId,
18	    url: payload.url ?? null,
19	    actor: payload.actor ?? null,
20	    data: payload.data,
21	    updated_from: payload.updatedFrom ?? null,
22	  };
23	}
24	
25	/**
26	 * Webhook タイムスタンプが許容範囲内か検証する（リプレイ攻撃防止）
27	 */
28	export function validateTimestamp(webhookTimestamp: number): boolean {
29	  const now = Date.now();
30	  const diff = Math.abs(now - webhookTimestamp);
31	  return diff <= TIMESTAMP_TOLERANCE_MS;
32	}
33	
34	const linear = new Hono<{ Bindings: Env }>();
35	
36	linear.post("/", async (c) => {
37	  const signature = c.req.header("Linear-Signature");
38	  if (!signature) {
39	    return c.json({ error: "Missing signature" }, 401);
40	  }
41	
42	  const rawBody = await c.req.text();
43	
44	  const secret = await c.env.LINEAR_WEBHOOK_SECRET.get();
45	  const isValid = await verifySignature(rawBody, signature, secret);
46	  if (!isValid) {
47	    return c.json({ error: "Invalid signature" }, 401);
48	  }
49	
50	  let payload: LinearWebhookPayload;
51	  try {
52	    payload = JSON.parse(rawBody) as LinearWebhookPayload;
53	  } catch {
54	    return c.json({ error: "Invalid JSON payload" }, 400);
55	  }
56	
57	  if (!validateTimestamp(payload.webhookTimestamp)) {
58	    return c.json({ error: "Timestamp out of range" }, 401);
59	  }
60	
61	  const event = normalizePayload(payload);
62	
63	  await c.env.STREAM.send([event]);
64	
65	  return c.json({ success: true }, 200);
66	});
67	
68	export default linear;
69
